Then I'm working through the author's questions on Definition 9, starting with why we need the pre-action subalgebra rather than just condition (2). The issue is that condition (2) alone—pinning action-conditional beliefs and values—leaves the unconditional distribution largely undetermined because actions aren't necessarily exhaustive, so P_s(X) can vary widely even when all the conditionals are fixed. Clause (1) is needed to constrain the unconditional calibration on ℰ₀, especially in degenerate cases where the agent has zero credence in all but one action.

Without clause (1), an agent could maintain wildly incorrect pre-action beliefs (about the coin flip, the lesion, the announcement) while still satisfying clause (2)'s action-conditional constraints. But actually, when the state assigns full probability to the action menu and all positive actions have positive probability under the observation, clause (2) plus the law of total probability forces the unconditional belief to be a mixture of the action-conditionals weighted by the state's own action credences—that's the real constraint clause (2) provides.

Clause (1) pins this mixture to the *true* mixture, weighted by actual action frequencies. The difference emerges exactly when the state's action-credences diverge from reality, which is precisely the freedom masking grants. Without clause (1), miscalibrated self-credences could contaminate the pre-action worldview: believing I'll probably smoke plus correct conditionals about lesion-given-smoking could lead me to believe I probably have the lesion. Clause (1) quarantines this contamination by forcing pre-action facts to match reality regardless of the agent's self-opinion. The design works cleanly when pre-action facts are action-independent (like a fair coin), but leaks occur with action-correlated pre-action facts—exactly the predictor problems where the transfer depends on what the agent observes and does.

In Newcomb's problem with opaque boxes, the screening lemma applies: the conditional probability of the box being full doesn't actually vary with the agent's action because the tree structure screens them apart. The real question is whether action-conditionals ever differ from marginals under our semantics. With independent redraws, everything screens out—the live draw is independent of the hypothetical one, so the conditional collapses to the marginal. This suggests that under our framework, clause (2)'s conditional might never actually diverge from the marginal in the first place.

But there's a subtlety with path-position: when the same action event appears at multiple decision points (different branches), conditioning on the observation and action reweights the mixture over those points. The honest answer involves two key arguments: first, the quarantine argument—clause (2) only pins a self-weighted mixture while clause (1) pins the objective marginal unconditionally, and they differ precisely when conditionals vary in the action and masking is needed to correct wrong self-weights. Second, the degenerate-guard problem—clause (2) is guarded by positive probability conditions, so for a deterministic or dogmatic agent, clause (2) becomes nearly vacuous while clause (1) keeps something pinned unconditionally, even for beliefs about untaken actions.

The clean vacuity witness is when an agent's credence spreads over actions that never occur under the deterministic policy: those never-taken actions have unconstrained conditionals, so pre-action beliefs become partially arbitrary without clause (1). Clause (1) blocks exactly this corruption. Methodologically, clause (1) is what makes masked a genuine restriction of the strict framework—under strict conditions, the conditional probabilities become derivable from the observation and action alone.

Now for clause (2): it pins the conditional beliefs P_s(X|a) to match the objective conditional ν(X|O∧a) for all X in the full belief space ℰ, not just ℰ₀. The reason for using conditionals rather than joints is that pinning the joint P_s(X∧a) would force the action marginal P_s(a) to match the observation-conditional policy, which reintroduces self-transparency and defeats the whole point of masking. Conditionals are the largest pinning that leaves the action weights free—the state decomposes as a mixture over actions where masking frees the weights but pins each component. And we need the full ℰ in clause (2) because once you condition on the action, there's no remaining self-knowledge to protect; the objective act-conditional is exactly what an evidential deliberator needs to evaluate the action.

The two guards—ν(O∧a) > 0 and P_s(a) > 0—ensure both conditionals exist; the first prevents undefined statistics and the second prevents undefined conditional values after the domain revert. Notably, their independence allows false certainty where the agent assigns zero subjective probability to an action that's objectively possible, which is the whole point of the pending decision.

For the value part, clause (2) pins V_s(X∧a) rather than some hypothetical conditional V_s(X|a) because Jeffrey's framework has no primitive conditional desirability—Jeffrey's conditional value is exactly the conjunction V(X∧a), matching the pattern used for dogmatic states. This is also what EDT consumes: the act value V_s(a) equals the clause with X=⊤, so clause (2) directly pins EDT's act values to the objective conditional expected payoffs. The punchline is that a masked-calibrated EDT agent evaluates acts by their true conditional expected payoffs while remaining genuinely uncertain about what it will do.

Now checking consistency: the condition "both sides defined" requires P_s(X∧a) > 0 on the left and μ(λ⊨X∧a∧O) > 0 on the right, and under clause (2) these decompose correctly through the guards to ensure ν(X∧O∧a) > 0. The averaging property also holds—for disjoint conjunctions X∧a and Y∧a, the weights P_s(X∧a) ∝ P_s(a)ν(X|O∧a) match the μ-weights, so the partition argument works. But I'm noticing something odd: clause (1) pins P_s on the base events ℰ₀, yet there's no corresponding unconditional V-clause—V_s(X) for non-action-decomposable events like V_s(coin=T) is left free. That seems asymmetric. Pinning unconditional values would require averaging over actions, which would reintroduce self-weights into the values themselves, so I deliberately left it free to mirror the P-side's freedom. But wait, clause (1) actually does pin P on ℰ₀, so why doesn't V have an unconditional clause? The issue is that unconditional V_s(X) represents the agent's expected payoff given X, which depends on what the agent will do given X—pinning it would smuggle self-knowledge back in through the values.

This is actually deliberate and worth noting: the absence of an unconditional V-clause in the masked definition is precisely because such values encode policy expectations, creating a value-shaped leak of self-knowledge that masking must avoid. Looking back at v1, the masked definition had the same structure, so this observation connects forward to how Prop 6 uses the masked framework and how Prop 11 applies clause (2) with X={k}.

Definition 9, clause 1's necessity. Two arguments, one cheap and decisive, one structural. The cheap one is a vacuity witness: clause 2 is guarded by $\nu(O_d \wedge a) > 0$, so never-taken actions have *unconstrained* conditionals — and masking exists precisely to let the state spread credence over actions it won't take. Pre-action beliefs get corrupted through exactly the self-uncertainty masking grants — check it, it's three lines. The structural version: even where clause 2 fully applies, clause-2-only pins $P_{s_d}$ on $\mathcal{E}_0$ to a *self-weighted* mixture $\sum_a P_{s_d}(a)\, \nu(\cdot \mid O_d \wedge a)$, whereas clause 1 pins it to the objectively weighted one; these differ whenever conditionals vary with the act and the state's self-credences are wrong — the very freedom being granted. So clause 1 is a quarantine: the agent's self-opinion may be anything, but it must not leak into its worldview. And it's what keeps masking on a dial with strict calibration: set $\mathcal{E}_0 = \mathcal{E}$ and clause 1 *is* strict clause 1 (with clause 2 then derivable), so masking interpolates by shrinking the quarantined algebra — clause-2-only wouldn't sit on that dial at all. The cost of the design is that $\mathcal{E}_0$ is modelling data (Q2).

Clause 2's anatomy, piece by piece. *Conditionals, not joints*: pinning $P_{s_d}(X \wedge a)$ would pin the marginal $P_{s_d}(a)$ and re-import the self-transparency masking exists to remove; the state's credence factors as weights times components, and clause 2 pins the components while freeing the weights. *Full $\mathcal{E}$ inside the conditional* (versus $\mathcal{E}_0$ in clause 1): once the act is given, there is no self-knowledge left to protect — conditioning on $a$ screens the agent's freedom, and the full act-conditional is exactly what an evidential deliberator consumes. *The two guards*: $\nu(O_d \wedge a) > 0$ because otherwise there is no statistic to equate (Definition 10's trembles are the repair for reachable zeros); $P_{s_d}(a) > 0$ because after the domain revert, $P_{s_d}(\cdot \mid a)$ and $V_{s_d}(X \wedge a)$ don't exist at subjectively-null acts. *The $V$-part as a conjunction*: JB has no primitive conditional desirability — Jeffrey's conditional value of $X$ given $a$ *is* $V(X \wedge a)$ — and with $X = \top$ the clause reads $V_{s_d}(a) = \mathbb{E}_\mu[r \mid \lambda \models a \wedge O_d]$: a masked-calibrated EDT agent's act values are the true conditional expected payoffs, while what it will *do* stays open. That is the clause's whole purpose in one sentence. Finally, an absence you should notice and that I believe is load-bearing rather than accidental: there is no unconditional $V$-clause on $\mathcal{E}_0$ — $P_{s_d}(\text{tails})$ is pinned but $V_{s_d}(\text{tails})$ is not — because the unconditional value of a pre-action fact encodes what the agent expects to *do* about it, so pinning it would smuggle self-knowledge back in through the value side.
